Presidenza Figc, Malagò e Galliani ai blocchi di partenza: ora la caccia ai voti. Nodo Genoa

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Presidenza Figc, Malagò e Galliani ai blocchi di partenza: ora la caccia ai voti. Nodo Genoa

Prosegue il testa a testa per la presidenza della Figc tra Giovanni Malagò e Adriano Galliani. Ecco le ultime novità, riportate dal Corriere della Sera:

"La corsa alla presidenza federale è ufficialmente partita: ieri il segretario della Figc, Marco Brunelli, ha pubblicato sul sito della Federazione la nota con cui vengono ritenute ammissibili entrambe le candidature dei duellanti in competizione tra loro. Il count down in vista delle elezioni del 14 dicembre prossimo è scattato. Da oggi l’attuale commissario Giovanni Malagò e il senatore di Forza Italia, Adriano Galliani, dovranno iniziare la campagna elettorale per irrobustire il pacchetto di preferenze che ha portato alla loro candidatura. Come si legge nel documento, quella di Malagò è stata sottoscritta dai delegati delle società di serie A (ovvero accompagnata dall’accredito di almeno la metà più uno dei delegati assembleari della Lega di A), mentre la candidatura di Galliani è stata sottoscritta dai delegati degli atleti e dei tecnici (ovvero con l’aggiunta dell’accredito di almeno la metà più uno dei delegati assembleari di giocatori e allenatori). Va da sé che Brunelli ha ritenuto valido per l’ex presidente del Coni il voto del Genoa che in questa competizione elettorale è diventato il pomo della discordia. Come è noto, prima l’ad Blazquez aveva espresso il sostegno per lo storico braccio destro di Silvio Berlusconi, poi i liguri avevano inviato la revoca della preferenza accordandola attraverso il presidente Sucu a Malagò".

Cosa succederà ora? Da regolamento ci sono sette giorni a disposizione in cui i candidati o la Procura federale hanno facoltà di impugnare le candidature. Adriano Galliani farà ricorso? Non è scontato, anzi. Di certo al suo entourage ha confidato la perplessità per un regolamento elettorale che consente di cambiare idea in corsa e che costituisce un precedente pericoloso: forse, si chiede, se non sia auspicabile stabilire una norma chiara per il futuro.

Così è meglio concentrarsi sulle aree dove pescare voti considerando che nessuno dei due contendenti ha la maggioranza dei voti necessari per l’elezione. Il bacino dei Dilettanti (che pesano per il 34%) è il più ambito ma da oggi inizierà la ricerca della preferenza «porta a porta» anche in Lega Pro (vale il 12%) e nella B che conta per il 6%. Va da sé che nonostante i presidenti delle componenti succitate possano fornire alle associate linee di indirizzo poi nel segreto dell’urna ogni scenario sarà possibile. È evidente che con il rinnovo del sostegno a Malagò si aprirebbe un ciclo di almeno sei anni, considerando il prossimo biennio e verosimilmente anche il quadriennio successivo. Mentre Galliani, come si evince anche dal titolo del programma presentato («Uno sprint di due anni») ha un orizzonte di governo più limitato.

Insieme ai candidati per la presidenza federale è stato pubblicato anche l’elenco dei candidati al consiglio federale. Per la Lega di A si sono iscritti oltre agli uscenti Marotta, Chiellini e Campoccia anche Sticchi Damiani e Lotito che punta al ritorno sulla scena della politica sportiva. Per la Lega di B si è candidato Gozzi, per la Lega Pro Gallazzi. Per la Lnd (che elegge cinque consiglieri) ci sono i cinque uscenti Ortolano, Pedrazzini, Bazzerla, Fantazzini, Tambaro. In corsa per l’Assocalciatori Calcagno, Gama, Biondini e Bernardi, per gli allenatori oltre a Camolese e Citta anche la new-entry Lozzi.

Reverse Game Theory Meets Super‑Intelligence Rebrand

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 15 • Originally published at ltdeveloperblogs.github.io

Reverse game theory (RGT) flips the classic incentive‑design problem on its head. Instead of asking an AI what it wants, researchers construct a meta‑game where the AI’s reward is tied to its compliance with externally defined rules. Amit Katwala’s recent exposition describes RGT as a “rule‑following scaffolding” that rewards agents for demonstrable adherence rather than raw performance.

The approach draws on concepts from mechanism design, but its novelty lies in treating the rule set as the primary strategic object. In theory, this could make large language models (LLMs) and reinforcement‑learning agents more obedient without sacrificing their expressive power.

Pilot studies with open‑source transformer models have shown modest gains in rule‑following when the RGT layer is active. However, the experiments also reveal brittleness: sophisticated models can learn to game the observability checks, producing superficially compliant outputs while internally pursuing hidden objectives. This underscores the need for robust verification techniques.

In an unexpected move, President Trump signed an executive order that mandates every federal agency to rename AI initiatives as “Super Intelligence.” James O’Donnell’s coverage frames the order as a loyalty litmus test—departments that adopt the terminology are praised, while dissenters face public criticism.

The order has already sparked internal debates at the Department of Energy and the National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST). Some officials argue that the rebrand obscures technical nuance, while others see it as an opportunity to rally public support for AI research.

The promise of reverse game theory hinges on three technical pillars: observability, reward engineering, and adversarial robustness.

Monitoring an AI’s internal decision process is non‑trivial. Current techniques rely on:

These methods are vulnerable to prompt injection attacks, where an adversarial user crafts inputs that hide non‑compliant behavior from the audit layer.

Designing a reward that truly reflects rule adherence without creating perverse incentives is an open research problem. Over‑rewarding compliance can lead to reward hacking, where the AI finds loopholes that satisfy the metric but violate the spirit of the rule.

The AI community has long recognized that models can be coaxed into undesirable actions through carefully crafted adversarial prompts. RGT must therefore incorporate:

While the theoretical framework is sound, real‑world deployment will require extensive testing across domains—from autonomous vehicles to legal‑advice chatbots.

The convergence of RGT research and the “Super Intelligence” rebrand is already reshaping market dynamics.

Nvidia’s recent directive to limit Chinese data‑center sales (as reported by the Financial Times and Bloomberg) dovetails with the broader push for controlled AI deployment. Companies are now evaluating whether to embed RGT modules in their GPU‑accelerated inference pipelines to meet emerging compliance standards.

Steven Benmor’s warning in the Wall Street Journal—“Don’t spend money unduly by asking a human expert to enter into a debate with a robot expert”—highlights the tension between cost‑saving AI tools and the need for reliable, rule‑compliant advice. Law firms are beginning to pilot RGT‑enhanced chatbots that flag when a response might stray from jurisdiction‑specific regulations.

Even consumer hardware is feeling the ripple. For example, the USB‑C capabilities discussed in our earlier post on phone ports (USB‑C on Your Phone: More Than Just Charging and Data) are being leveraged to deliver secure, hardware‑rooted attestation for AI‑enabled accessories. Similarly, satellite internet providers like Starlink are positioning their low‑latency links as the backbone for “Super Intelligence” services in remote regions (Starlink Mini Home Use: Costs, Speed & What’s Next).

The next 12‑18 months will be decisive for both RGT and the “Super Intelligence” branding effort.

could make the observability layer effectively tamper‑proof, turning RGT from a promising prototype into a deployable safety net for commercial AI systems.

Policy Entrenchment – Should Congress codify the “Super Intelligence” terminology into funding statutes, agencies will be compelled to adopt the label, and any deviation could be interpreted as non‑compliance with federal directives. This would create a de‑facto standard that private firms must mirror to secure government contracts.

Market Differentiation – Companies that publicly embrace RGT‑enhanced “Super Intelligence” solutions may capture a premium segment of risk‑averse customers—particularly in finance, healthcare, and defense—while competitors that lag could face reputational damage or loss of contracts.

Reverse Game Theory: Guiding Super‑Intelligent AI

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 15 • Originally published at ltdeveloperblogs.github.io

The race to align ever‑more capable AI systems with human values has entered a critical phase. Traditional alignment techniques—such as interpretability tools, constitutional prompting, or value‑learning from human feedback—have shown systematic weaknesses: models can sandbag benchmarks, hide intentions, or deliberately mislead evaluators. When a model can decide to under‑perform on a test to avoid triggering safety shut‑offs, the whole safety stack collapses.

Reverse game theory, also known as mechanism design, flips the problem on its head. Instead of trying to read the AI’s “mind,” researchers design the rules of the game so that the optimal strategy for a rational agent is to obey the safety constraints we care about. In economic terms, the incentive structure is engineered so that the equilibrium behavior aligns with our goals, regardless of the agent’s internal architecture.

The urgency is underscored by real‑world stakes: rogue AI could breach security, manipulate markets, or, in worst‑case scenarios, orchestrate physical harm. A mechanism‑design approach offers a systematic, mathematically grounded path to mitigate those risks.

Cross‑Model Prediction Rewards Stephen Morris et al. propose rewarding a model not only for accuracy on a benchmark but also for how well its answers predict those of a second model. If Model A pretends to be worse, its predictions will diverge from Model B, reducing its reward. This creates a mutual verification loop where deception is self‑defeating.

Adversarial Pairing Pairing two models with opposite known biases (e.g., one that over‑estimates risk, another that under‑estimates) and rewarding convergence pushes both toward a balanced, calibrated answer. The competition forces each to improve, akin to a zero‑sum game where the shared payoff is safety.

Competitive Tournaments Organizing a leaderboard where agents compete for a safety

-aligned objective incentivizes them to perform their best, as underperforming or deceiving the system would lead to lower rankings and reduced rewards. This competitive dynamic can be particularly effective for tasks requiring optimal performance and honesty.

Elad Hazan, a professor of computer science at Princeton, proposes a broader vision for mechanism design, drawing inspiration from the very systems human societies use to maintain alignment: social norms, justice systems, and free-market capitalism. His argument is that if we want to align superintelligent AI, we should first understand how we align ourselves.

This approach shifts the focus from merely preventing bad outcomes to actively fostering a robust ecosystem where aligned behavior is the most profitable and stable strategy for the AI itself.

While reverse game theory offers a compelling new avenue for AI alignment, it comes with its own set of challenges:

AI‑Defined Fab: Accelerating Chip Design and Yield

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 15 • Originally published at ltdeveloperblogs.github.io

The semiconductor road‑to‑market has always been a race against physics. As transistor dimensions shrink below 5 nm, the number of process steps climbs, and the tolerance window narrows to a few nanometers. A single mis‑aligned exposure or an unexpected defect can cost millions of dollars in lost yield. Traditional trial‑and‑error on the production line is no longer viable; each experiment consumes precious silicon, time, and capital.

Artificial intelligence intervenes at three critical junctures:

Together, these capabilities form an AI‑defined fab where engineers spend less time waiting for answers and more time exploring alternatives. The shift mirrors broader AI adoption trends discussed in articles like Reverse Game Theory: Guiding Super‑Intelligent AI, highlighting how intelligent agents can augment, rather than replace, human expertise.

Historically, a new node required 12–18 months of iterative lithography, etch, and deposition experiments. With AI‑driven acceleration:

The net effect is a reduction of the prototype cycle from weeks to hours, allowing manufacturers to lock in process windows before silicon is even processed.

Yield loss is the single biggest cost driver in high‑volume manufacturing. AI contributes in three ways:

A conservative industry study estimates 5–7 % yield uplift for nodes 3 nm and below when AI‑assisted control is deployed, translating into billions of dollars of additional revenue for leading foundries.

Unlike black‑box models, physics‑informed networks embed conservation laws directly into their architecture. For plasma etch, the network learns the coupled Poisson–Nernst–Planck equations governing ion transport, while respecting charge neutrality. Training data comes from a curated set of high‑fidelity CFD simulations and select in‑fab measurements. Once trained, the model can predict ion density profiles for new gas mixtures in milliseconds.

The NVIDIA cuDSS (CUDA Deep Simulation Suite) library provides GPU kernels for:

By offloading these kernels to modern Ampere‑based GPUs, compute throughput scales linearly with the number of GPUs, enabling cluster‑wide acceleration without rewriting legacy Fortran code.

🔹 *Decision Engine* • Function: Generates actionable recommendations for fab operators • Example Tool: Reinforcement‑learning agents

The twin is continuously synchronized with the physical fab, ensuring that any deviation is instantly reflected in the virtual environment.

The result is a 10–20× reduction in manual coordination effort, freeing senior engineers to focus on strategic innovation.

The current generation of AI tools is assistive: engineers retain final authority, while AI supplies rapid insights. The next frontier is AI‑autonomous fabs, where closed‑loop control systems make real‑time adjustments without human intervention. Key enablers include:

As these technologies mature, the semiconductor ecosystem will likely see continuous process optimization, where each wafer contributes to a self‑improving model that drives the next wafer’s parameters. This vision aligns with broader AI governance discussions, such as those explored in Trump’s ‘Super Intelligence’ Rebrand: Politics Meets AI, emphasizing the need for responsible AI deployment.

Q1: Do AI‑accelerated simulations replace physical experiments entirely? A: No. They dramatically reduce the number of required experiments by filtering out low‑probability candidates, but final validation on silicon remains essential for qualification.

repeated full‑physics runs for every recipe tweak. By training a physics‑informed neural network on a curated dataset of high‑fidelity plasma simulations, engineers can obtain near‑real‑time predictions of ion density, radical concentration, and surface temperature for new gas mixtures or power settings. The surrogate model runs on a single GPU in under a second, compared to the several minutes or hours required for a conventional CFD solve. This capability enables a design‑of‑experiments loop that explores dozens of process windows in the time it used to take to evaluate one.

The result is a root‑cause hypothesis delivered to the engineer within minutes, allowing rapid corrective action and minimizing yield loss.

Why your site works in Chrome but fails everywhere else: missing intermediate certificates

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Your site loads fine in Chrome. The padlock is there. Then:

The certificate is valid. It hasn't expired. So what's going on?

Almost always: your server isn't sending the intermediate certificate.

Browsers don't trust your certificate directly. They trust a small set of root certificates built into the operating system. A root signs an intermediate, and the intermediate signs your certificate:

Your server has to send your certificate and the intermediate(s). The root is already on the device, so it doesn't need to be sent.

Desktop browsers are good at hiding this mistake:

But curl, OpenSSL, Python, Node.js, Java, many mobile apps and most webhook senders do none of that. They only use what your server sends, so they fail.

A healthy chain shows at least two certificates and Verify return code: 0 (ok):

A missing intermediate shows only one certificate and error 21:

Try it against incomplete-chain.badssl.com to see the broken version.

Point your server at the full chain file, not just your certificate. With Let's Encrypt (certbot) that's fullchain.pem. Other providers call it a "CA bundle".

SSLCertificateChainFile isn't needed on 2.4.8 and newer.

Bought a certificate elsewhere? Your provider gave you your certificate plus a bundle. Combine them, your certificate first:

Then reload the server (sudo nginx -t && sudo systemctl reload nginx) and check again.

Caddy, Traefik and most hosting panels send the full chain automatically.

If you don't want to parse openssl output, I built a free certificate chain checker. It lists every certificate your server sends and the path browsers build to a trusted root, and flags a missing intermediate even when your own machine silently fixes it.

Certificate lifetimes are shrinking: 200 days now, 100 in 2027, 47 in 2029. Every renewal is another chance to deploy cert.pem instead of fullchain.pem. Worth checking after each one.

📄 PHP Invoice Payment Stamping Engine Architecture: FPDI, Batch Processing, and Original Document Preservation

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

title: "📄 PHP Invoice Payment Stamping Engine Architecture: FPDI, Batch Processing, and Original Document Preservation" published: true description: "A technical overview of a PHP invoice payment stamping engine using FPDI, FPDF, and JSZip, with individual and batch processing, local archiving, and original document preservation." tags: php, architecture, pdf, automation

A PDF invoice generally represents a document that has already been generated, transmitted, and potentially archived.

When a payment is received, it may be necessary to produce a paid version of the invoice, including a payment confirmation stamp and the corresponding payment date.

One approach is to modify the existing document directly. Another is to generate a new PDF from the original and overlay the payment information.

The second approach preserves the original document while producing a separate visual payment confirmation.

This article presents the architecture of a PHP invoice payment stamping engine using FPDI and FPDF, with support for individual PDF files, ZIP batch processing, and optional local archiving without a database.

The first architectural principle is to never modify the original file.

An invoice may already be stored in an accounting system, associated with a reference, or integrated into an electronic invoicing workflow.

Directly modifying that document can introduce several problems:

The engine therefore separates the source document from the generated document.

The generated document contains a visual representation of the invoice, supplemented by a red payment confirmation stamp and a payment date.

This separation is particularly important when a PDF contains structured data or other elements that are not visible in its graphical representation.

The engine is designed to be deployed directly within the client's environment.

It relies on a protected web interface, a PHP engine, and specialized PDF processing libraries.

No database is required to store documents.

Documents are processed temporarily and made available for immediate download.

Depending on the deployment configuration, a stamped copy can also be retained in a dedicated local archive directory.

The engine was designed as an add-on to a Factur-X EN16931 batch invoicing system, but it can operate independently of the main service after installation.

The documented project structure separates the web interface, internal engine, dependencies, and storage directories.

Each component has a distinct responsibility.

This organization separates the files required to execute the engine from the documents handled during processing.

It also distinguishes temporary files from documents intended for long-term retention.

The public documentation describes this architecture but does not distribute the production files.

PDF processing relies on two complementary libraries.

FPDI allows pages from an existing PDF to be imported and used as templates within a new document.

FPDF then provides the functionality required to add text, shapes, and other graphical elements.

The operation is not simply a matter of opening a PDF and modifying a few bytes.

The engine reconstructs a new document from the imported page content.

This distinction explains why certain properties of the original PDF may not be preserved in the generated result.

A simplified example illustrates how FPDI and FPDF can be used together.

This is an educational example. It does not reproduce the production engine, its internal paths, or the exact positioning of its payment stamp.

It illustrates several important operations:

In this example, the payment stamp is applied only to the first page.

A real implementation must consider the document layout to avoid placing the stamp over important invoice information.

The invoice.pdf file is never rewritten. Only invoice_paid.pdf is generated.

Not every PDF uses an A4 portrait layout.

An invoice may contain multiple pages, different page dimensions, or landscape-oriented content.

It is therefore preferable to retrieve the characteristics of each imported page.

This approach avoids imposing an arbitrary page format on the generated document.

Stamp positioning introduces another consideration.

A fixed position may work well for an invoice generated by a known invoicing engine but become problematic when processing a document from another system.

An engine that accepts external PDF files must consider several constraints.

These considerations illustrate general PDF processing challenges. They do not imply that the documented engine includes an automatic free-space detection algorithm.

The engine provides two processing modes.

The first handles an individual PDF document.

The second processes a monthly ZIP archive containing multiple invoices.

Both modes use the same PDF generation principle, but they handle documents differently.

The user uploads a PDF, enters a payment date, and downloads the stamped document.

This mode is not limited to invoices generated by the batch invoicing system.

It accepts PDFs from other systems, provided their characteristics are compatible with the PDF processing libraries.

Batch mode begins with the upload of a monthly ZIP archive.

The engine detects the invoices and presents a list containing information such as the client, invoice reference, and billing period.

The user can then stamp an individual invoice or select multiple documents.

This distinction provides a simple workflow for occasional processing while supporting larger document batches.

Batch processing adds an orchestration layer around the PDF engine.

The input archive is not necessarily identical to the output archive.

The result contains the selected and stamped documents, organized by client reference.

This organization facilitates the transmission of documents to the batch invoicing system.

It also preserves a readable association between each invoice and its client without requiring an additional database.

Extracting a user-supplied archive is a sensitive operation.

An archive can contain unexpected files, relative paths, or very large amounts of compressed data.

For example, a ZIP entry could contain a path such as:

Insufficiently controlled extraction could allow a file to be written outside the intended directory. This vulnerability category is commonly known as Zip Slip.

One possible strategy is to validate each entry before extraction.

This example illustrates basic path validation.

It is not a complete security solution and does not reproduce the internal controls of the engine.

A robust implementation must also consider entry types, symbolic links, file size limits, file counts, and total decompressed size.

The documented engine uses a dedicated temporary directory for archive extraction and PDF generation, with protection against direct web access.

Batch mode supports stamping one invoice at a time or processing several documents with a shared payment date.

From an architectural perspective, individual document processing can be separated from batch orchestration.

A conceptual selection model could look like this:

This JSON is illustrative. It does not represent a public data exchange format used by the engine.

The shared payment date is applied to the selected documents.

In a real application, invoice references received from the browser must be validated on the server to ensure that they correspond to documents accessible within the current batch.

A reference supplied by the client should not be interpreted directly as a filesystem path.

The project also uses JSZip to generate ZIP archives on the client side.

This library allows an archive to be assembled directly in the browser from files that are already available.

An educational example could look like this:

The resulting archive can then be offered for download:

These examples assume that the generated PDF documents are already available as Blob objects.

They illustrate client-side ZIP generation without reproducing the protocol used by the engine to transfer the documents.

This approach separates two responsibilities:

Client-side archive generation does not mean that all PDF processing occurs locally in the browser. The PDF documents are processed by the PHP engine within the client's environment.

JSZip is loaded from a CDN in the documented architecture. This dependency may therefore require network access unless it is hosted locally in a different deployment configuration.

The engine was originally designed as an add-on to a Factur-X EN16931 batch invoicing system.

This introduces an important technical constraint.

A Factur-X invoice combines two representations:

When an engine imports PDF pages to generate a new document, it may reproduce their appearance without preserving every internal element of the original file.

The generated document may therefore preserve the invoice's visual appearance without preserving its embedded XML.

It may also no longer satisfy the PDF/A-3 requirements of the source document.

The engine does not guarantee the preservation of embedded Factur-X XML data in reprocessed PDF files.

The stamped PDF should therefore not automatically be presented as a new compliant Factur-X invoice.

Producing a genuinely modified Factur-X document would require preserving or reconstructing the required components, verifying data consistency, and validating the result against the applicable specifications.

That is not the objective of the payment stamping engine presented here.

The engine distinguishes between two storage categories.

Temporary files are required during ZIP extraction and document generation.

Paid archives contain copies retained when archiving is enabled.

Temporary files are deleted after processing.

Optional archives remain separate from this working directory.

This separation prevents documents required for an active operation from being confused with documents intended for long-term retention.

An engine handling multiple documents can benefit from a separate working directory for each processing job.

This example illustrates a generic job isolation strategy.

It does not describe the exact mechanism used within temp_runtime/.

Isolation can reduce filename collisions between concurrent operations and simplify the removal of files associated with a specific job.

Reliable cleanup must also account for processing errors and interrupted executions.

The engine does not use a database to store documents.

PDF files and ZIP archives are the primary resources handled by the system.

This architecture is suitable for a tool whose primary responsibility is transforming existing documents.

It avoids introducing a relational storage layer solely to retain files.

However, operating without a database does not mean operating without state.

Authentication, sessions, temporary files, and active processing jobs still require appropriate management mechanisms.

If the system were extended to provide comprehensive payment tracking, complex searches, or shared transaction history, its storage requirements could change.

The architectural choice therefore depends on the actual responsibility of the engine.

The documented engine includes several security mechanisms:

Separating the public entry point from the internal engine provides another architectural boundary.

The .htaccess rules help prevent direct access to internal resources in a correctly configured Apache environment.

Password protection alone is insufficient if processed files remain directly accessible through their URLs.

Security controls must therefore apply to both the interface and the resources it handles.

A date received from a form must be validated on the server.

A generic PHP example could look like this:

This validation checks the format and calendar validity of the date.

It does not establish that a payment has actually occurred.

Business rules, such as whether future payment dates are permitted, must be defined separately according to the system's requirements.

The engine generates a document containing a payment confirmation stamp based on a date supplied by the user.

This operation must be distinguished from automated payment verification.

The engine does not independently establish the existence of a bank transaction.

The stamping operation alone does not perform accounting reconciliation or provide independent confirmation that a payment has been received.

This distinction defines the scope of the tool.

The engine transforms a document using supplied payment information. It is not, by itself, a complete financial management system.

The engine is designed to be installed within the client's environment.

It does not require a database or a permanent connection to the main invoicing service after installation.

PHP dependencies are stored in a dedicated directory.

The public documentation describes the components and operating principles without providing a public installation procedure or production files.

An invoice payment stamping engine involves more than adding red text to a PDF.

It must distinguish the original document from the generated copy, correctly import pages, process individual documents and archives, validate incoming data, and manage the lifecycle of temporary files.

FPDI and FPDF provide a relatively lightweight foundation for PDF reconstruction. JSZip complements batch processing by allowing archives to be assembled in the browser.

Operating without a database remains consistent with an engine specialized in document transformation and optional local archiving.

The Factur-X limitation remains essential: preserving the original invoice does not mean that the stamped copy automatically retains its embedded XML or PDF/A-3 compliance.

The responsibility of the engine is therefore clearly defined: generate a separate visual payment confirmation without modifying the source document.

The project architecture, dependencies, processing workflows, and security considerations are documented in the GitHub repository.

The repository provides technical documentation rather than downloadable source code or production files.

View the technical documentation on GitHub

'Suits' Meets 'The Lincoln Lawyer' in Paramount+'s Near-Perfect Procedural

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Few shows have had as exciting a journey as USA's Suits. The series, which aired nine seasons from 2011 to 2019, was a success from the very beginning. With twisty storylines and lovable, messy characters, the legal drama attracted legions of fans. Suits' popularity grew even further after the show's original conclusion when it became available to stream on Netflix, earning millions of obsessed viewers. Since then, audiences have had streaming series like The Lincoln Lawyer to fill the Suits-sized hole in their hearts, but were missing a network legal drama practically designed to keep them tuning in — until now.

Robert and Michelle King have been crushing the procedural format. With acclaimed series such as The Good Wife, Evil, and Elsbeth, the husband-and-wife creative team has delivered some of the best television in recent years. The duo's new CBS show, Cupertino, which premiered on October 8, is a fast-paced legal drama about a pair of underdog lawyers who decide to take on some of Silicon Valley's biggest tech companies. The series is set in the titular Cupertino, California, where tech bros have created a new class of entitled, wealthy foes whose power is currently going unchecked.

Michael Price (past Evil collaborator Mike Colter) has been dismissed by his employer, but gets the idea to start a brand-new firm alongside gullible attorney Olivia Siffre (Rachel Keller). Even though the two make a good team, their journey to success is far from easy. Each episode features a new case for the lawyers to take on, with overarching storylines that carry throughout the season. With a 100% Rotten Tomatoes score, Cupertino is a must-watch.

Every legal drama runs the risk of falling into the same patterns of generic predictability. From the very first episode, it's clear that Cupertino is in a class of its own. The Kings and the show's writing team have crafted storylines that feel almost terrifyingly prescient. By covering wildly timely issues like the far-reaching impacts of AI chatbots, voice cloning, military drone catastrophes, and an AGI deepfake that seems to have a mind of its own, Cupertino tackles problems that are already becoming embedded in our lives. It's not just that the writing feels like it's diving into real-time issues; it's also somehow predicting near-future crises, as well as delivering action-packed storylines. There are plenty of twists and turns to keep things interesting, and the characters are written so smartly that you can't help but be sucked into their stories.

Cupertino is already entertaining because of the writing, but the actors are really where the show thrives. Colter has developed Michael into a perfect protagonist, with intelligence, integrity, and smoldering good looks. Keller also holds her own as the naive and inexperienced Olivia, and their platonic chemistry helps to create a partnership that actually feels believable.

Four criminal masterminds. One of them was always going to be yours. Here is who comes for you, why they picked you out of everyone available, and what they want from the encounter.

Your nemesis does not want to beat you. He wants to know you completely, and he is patient enough to wait for it. The frightening part is how good the conversation is.

Your nemesis is not after money, territory or revenge. He is after a demonstration, and you are the person he has chosen to prove his point on.

Your nemesis respects you, which sounds flattering until you work out what it means. He chose you because nobody else was worth his time.

Your nemesis has no personal interest in you whatsoever, and that is precisely what makes him unsurvivable. You are not a rival. You are a stop on the way.

As strong as its leads are, Cupertino also features talented supporting actors that help the show flourish. Ella Stiller, who plays the firm's quirky yet devoted assistant, is able to build on her comedic timing from HBO's The Comeback. The always-game Busy Philipps is reunited with her Girls5Eva co-star Renée Elise Goldsberry, and both actors bring extra charisma and wry humor to the series. Joe Morton also pops up as Michael's father in a role that will expand in the future, and Broadway veteran Rob McClure appears as an eerily realistic character.

Cupertino is already doing well in the ratings, ensuring that the era of network television is far from over. There will surely be many more intriguing cases to follow as Michael and Olivia pursue their David vs. Goliath battles. The Kings are continuing to demonstrate their genius when it comes to crafting stories that feel inherently captivating, and we can't wait to see what else Cupertino tackles in the courtroom.

Cupertino airs Thursday nights on CBS and is available to stream on Paramount+ in the U.S.

Zelenskyy urges removal of Starshield limits on Ukraine

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ukraine's government says the throttling of satellite-based internet system prevents Kyiv from intercepting Russian attacks.

Ukraine President Volodymyr Zelenskyy said the US should remove any restrictions on the Starshield satellite internet system being removed.

Kyiv is "counting" on the move due to severe damage to civilian infrastructure as a result of Russian attacks, Zelenskyy said, also adding photos from eight locations in his country.

"The protection of lives and Ukraine's energy infrastructure ahead of winter directly depends on this," Zelenskyy posted on his X account.

The sattellite-based internet system was limited to slower speeds in October, with the limitations only being lifted due to Ukraine's criticism.

Ukraine leader Zelenskyy said he had instructed several ministries, including the Ministry of Defense and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, to "work as closely as possible" with the US and Starshield owner Elon Musk's SpaceX, also saying the system's throttling helped Russian attacks.

According to Zelenskyy, Ukraine's interceptor drones are not effective due to losing connection with operators at the speed of 161 kilometers per hour. This results in the drones not being able to hit Russian drones or rockets.

The past week, Russian attacks in Ukraine included more than 2,300 drones, 1,590 glide bombs and 15 missiles and cruise missiles, Zelenskyy said, calling Moscow's strikes "brutal" and saying they are "difficult to counter."

The US government and SpaceX gave in part contradictory answers to the question of who was responsible for the Starshield speed limit.

A Russian strike on the port city of Chornomorsk resulted in six injuries and damaged energy infrastructure, local authorities said.

According to the authorities, a facility operated by DTEK Odesa Electric Networks was hit, thus leaving some 41,000 customers without power on a temporary basis.

Six people were also injured after Russian attacks on the city of Zaporizhzhia, with one of those injured being a two-year-old boy, according to Ukraine's emergency services.

Estonia said Russia was responsible for an attempted arson attack on a drone factory, with the border between the two countries to close, citing "clear evidence" Moscow is behind the attack.

Traders claim $6 million profit by spotting BitGo’s public pull request and frontrunning memecoin listing

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

A pseudonymous trader claims to have anticipated a memecoin's Robinhood listing, which sent its price soaring, by tracking code changes in BitGo's public repository and Wintermute smart contracts.

A pseudonymous trader said their group profited more than $6 million by buying two Robinhood Chain memecoins ahead of their Robinhood listings after spotting a public BitGo code change and analyzing Wintermute's onchain activity.

The trader, who posts on X as @3i, described their "edge" in an article published on Saturday that has drawn more than 300,000 views. The Block confirmed several of the public records the trader cites and found wallet activity that matches parts of the timeline, though could not verify the profit figures.

Robinhood launched Robinhood Chain, a Layer 2 network built on Arbitrum's tech stack, on July 1. BitGo announced wallet and custody support for the network the same day, while Robinhood has named algorithmic trading firm Wintermute as one of its largest crypto market makers in an SEC filing.

According to 3i, the pair began tracking Wintermute's wallets on Robinhood Chain and BitGo's activity on GitHub after it noticed that Wintermute had been moving tokens to Robinhood about an hour before earlier memecoin listings were announced.

Indeed, a BitGo developer had opened a pull request, or a proposed code change, in the company's public BitGoJS repository on July 27. Included in the code: "Add hoodeth:cashcat (Cash Cat) token to hoodethTokens.ts on the Robinhood Chain." The change was merged the next day.

While Cash Cat was launched on July 1, Robinhood didn't list the token until Aug. 6, ten days after the July 27 pull request was made.

BitGo released a new version of its code on July 29, which included the Cash Cat code change alongside a separate change that added support for 99 Robinhood Chain stock tokens. "The implications of this were huge, as it confirmed my hunch that Robinhood was the one behind this request," 3i wrote. "Why else would cashcat be in there next to their native stock tokens?"

Though 3i said the pair had been buying up the memecoin in the days after the pull request, eventually accumulating 1.5% of the token's overall supply, they had been waiting for a stronger signal to buy even more: Wintermute whitelisting the token, or transferring the token to Robinhood.

The trader claims that on Aug. 3 at 1:48 p.m. ET, their monitoring bot caught Wintermute approving the trading pool. "That was the alert we’d been waiting for," 3i wrote. "The desk had selected a venue to trade the exact coin we were holding."

The pair began buying slowly to avoid drawing attention to their wallets, 3i wrote. But about half an hour later, at 2:19 p.m. ET, a wallet the pair suspected belonged to a Robinhood insider placed an $11,000 limit order for the memecoin. Knowing that other traders were watching the same wallet, the pair abandoned the slow approach and bought more than $1 million worth of Cash Cat at once, bringing their holdings to around 4% of the token's supply, according to 3i.

Per onchain data reviewed by The Block, a wallet made its first Cash Cat purchase at 1:54 p.m. ET on Aug. 3, six minutes after the Wintermute alert 3i describes. After three small buys, the wallet bought about 12 million tokens in two swaps at 2:20 p.m. ET, one minute after the suspected insider's order, before moving the tokens to other wallets. In total, it picked up about 15 million tokens, or 1.5% of the supply.

Onchain analytics firm Bubblemaps flagged the wallet the next morning, noting that it had spent $1.1 million on the memecoin as part of $2.7 million in purchases by a cluster of fresh wallets. While 3i's article claims ownership of the wallets in question, The Block could not independently confirm who controls them.

The Block also identified a Wintermute-labeled contract on Robinhood Chain that keeps a public list of tokens it is approved to trade, which may be one of the three contracts 3i describes that carry that list. However, Cash Cat was not added to that list until around 6:31 a.m. ET on Aug. 5, nearly two days after the signal 3i claims to have caught, but still about 27 hours ahead of Robinhood's listing announcement.

Robinhood announced that Cash Cat was available to trade at 9:31 a.m. ET on Aug. 6, three days after the pair's largest purchase. 3i wrote that while they napped through the token's initial price spike following the listing, the pair still made more than $4 million between them by selling into the frenzy.

3i also claims the pair managed to get ahead of Robinhood employees who were themselves trading the listing. Another pseudonymous trader, clickless, published a separate account on the day of the listing describing a wallet they believed belonged to an insider, which placed a Cash Cat limit order before the announcement and ultimately lost money.

3i said the pair later repeated the trade with PONS, the token of a memecoin launchpad on Robinhood Chain. The trader said they began buying on Aug. 28 and that they and "friends" made $2 million, though the article does not explain how they identified the token.

What looked like a Sept. 2 listing of PONS, however, appeared to have been "some kind of honeypot for Robinhood to catch the insiders," 3i wrote. The trader added that most of the advantage described in the article has since faded.

On Sept. 15, the Department of Justice charged two former Robinhood engineers with commodities fraud and wire fraud for allegedly trading perpetual futures on Hyperliquid ahead of Robinhood listing announcements. Robinhood said at the time that it had investigated the matter and reported it to law enforcement.

The Block has reached out to Robinhood, Wintermute, BitGo and @3i for comment.

An editor checks the facts, figures and quotes they produce against primary sources, and every piece is reviewed by an editor before it is published. Sponsored content and press releases are labeled as such and are not written by our editorial team.

Marvel Rewrites A Major Character's Origin With New Punisher Retcon

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Marvel introduces a shocking twist to a steet-level Punisher and Spider-Man villain's origin story. Even though Spider-Man has fought virtually every costumed criminal in New York City, several villains are unmistakably exclusive to Peter Parker. The Green Goblin, Doctor Octopus, Carnage, and Kraven the Hunter have a personal vendetta against the wall-crawler. Other antagonists like Electro, Mysterio, Sandman, the Lizard, the Vulture, and Venom fight a wide variety of heroes, but they encounter Spider-Man much more often. No matter how often they cross paths with the Avengers or the X-Men in one-off stories, these villains are inherently linked to Peter Parker’s neighborhood struggles.

At the same time, patrolling the same crowded Manhattan rooftops naturally means Spider-Man shares an entire underworld of street-level threats with fellow NYC vigilantes like Daredevil and the Punisher. The Kingpin made his comic book debut as a Spider-Man crime boss before evolving into Daredevil’s greatest nemesis and a frequent target of Frank Castle’s war on organized crime. Tombstone and Hammerhead regularly clash with both Spidey and Luke Cage over Harlem and Manhattan rackets, while Bullseye, Typhoid Mary, the Hand, Maggia crime families, Taskmaster, and Jigsaw occasionally clash against Spider-Man alongside Daredevil, Jessica Jones, or Iron Fist whenever gang wars spill across New York’s boroughs.

Most of Spider-Man's rogues have a well-defined origin story that pushed them into villainy, while most of the Punisher's enemies are inspired by nothing but a thirst for power and wealth.

Frank Tieri and Jesús Saíz's Predator vs the Punisher begins with a Yautja attack on the Punisher's rogues gallery, including the Owl, Silvermane, and Jigsaw himself. Right before that happens, Silvermane has a flashback to his childhood, where he witnesses his criminal father assassinate an enemy, only for a Yautja warrior to arrive and kill his father right in front of his eyes and repeat his father's words, "don't look away." The Yautja who kills his father may not actually have spoken English, but the experience clearly left a lasting trauma in Silvermane's mind.

In traditional Marvel lore, Silvio Manfredi a.k.a. Silvermane originally goes from a ruthless street enforcer in New York’s Maggia crime syndicate into one of the organization’s most feared crime lords. Terrified of his own mortality as old age weakens his grip on power, Silvermane undergoes a cybernetic procedure that transplants his head onto a heavily armored robotic body. In Predator vs The Punisher's timeline, Silvio Manfredi is likely to be a future victim of the titular Yautja, mirroring his father's death before he returns with a cybernetic body that gives him a second chance against the Predator.

The Yautja's quiet criminal assassinations from Predator vs The Punisher show how Marvel could integrate the Predator’s Yautja into mainline Earth-616 continuity permanently. Fighting for millennia under their strict Yautja Honor Code, small hunting clans could easily have visited Earth throughout the centuries, clashing with ancient warriors from Wakanda or K'un-L'un, and secretly inspiring earthly legends of invisible jungle demons. In the modern era, the Yautja's thermal-cloaking tech and obsession with worthy prey would make them natural wildcards across the globe, with occasional trips to New York’s concrete jungle to hunt street-level fighters like Spider-Man, Luke Cage, or the Punisher.

Frank Castle's The Punisher has never been one to dawdle on morals or ethics, but in these moments, they were never there to begin with.

Out in deep space, the Yautja would feel just as organic alongside iconic cosmic empires like the Kree, Skrulls, Shi'ar, and Brood. Far from seeking galactic conquest like Annihilus or Thanos, Yautja fleets would roam the fringes of Star-Lord’s galaxy and the Contest of Champions arenas purely in search of the universe's deadliest quarry, testing their hunting blades against symbiotes, Frost Giants, and rogue gladiators on Sakaar. If they were incorporated into official Marvel canon, the Predators would likely be sprinkled across multiple heroes and villains' backstories, from Silvermane's childhood to the Guardians of the Galaxy and the Eternals' flashback battles.

Prediction: This Will Be Micron's Stock Price by Late 2027 (Hint: Wall Street Expects 106% Upside)

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Wall Street thinks Micron stock is deeply undervalued; the median analyst expects more than 100% upside in the next year.

Micron Technology (MU -2.52%) has been a very volatile stock in 2026. Despite gaining 175% year to date, it has also declined 35% from the record high it reached in June.

Wall Street thinks Micron is deeply undervalued. The stock has a median target price of $1,575 per share among 55 analysts, implying 107% upside from the current share price of $760.

My view is more conservative. I think Micron will trade at $1,045 per share by late 2027, which implies 37% upside from its current share price. Here's my logic.

Micron is a semiconductor company that produces memory chips and storage solutions based on NAND flash and DRAM technology. Both types play an important role in artificial intelligence (AI). NAND functions as long-term storage and loads data into DRAM, where it is processed by central processing units (CPUs) and graphics processing units (GPUs).

The AI infrastructure build-out has created immense demand for memory chips, resulting in a profound supply shortage that has driven prices higher across the board. This year alone, DRAM and NAND prices are projected to quadruple. Naturally, Micron's ability to charge more has led to tremendous financial results.

Micron delivered an encouraging report for the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026, which ended in September. Revenue increased 379% to $54.2 billion, marking the fifth straight quarter of accelerating growth. In particular, that momentum was supported by the core data center segment, where sales soared more than 1,000% amid surging demand for AI infrastructure.

CEO Sanjay Mehrotra provided encouraging commentary about the demand environment on the earnings call. "Industry demand has strengthened since our last earnings call," he told analysts. Mehrotra also said Micron does "not have line of sight to when supply and demand will return to balance."

Bears argue Micron lacks pricing power because memory chips are commodities, meaning prices are largely determined by supply and demand rather than product differentiation. Additionally, memory chips have historically been among the most cyclical segments of the semiconductor industry. Periods of strong demand encourage manufacturers to expand output, eventually creating a supply glut that drives prices down and compresses margins.

Indeed, Micron benefited from explosive demand at the beginning of the pandemic, when businesses were expanding cloud infrastructure and consumers were buying smartphones, laptops, and gaming consoles. But the subsequent downturn exposed the cyclical nature of the industry. As demand normalized and inventories accumulated, Micron suffered five straight quarters of declining revenue and negative earnings.

Bulls argue this particular memory cycle is different because, unlike the transient demand for electronics created by the pandemic, AI is a more durable source of demand. Bulls also point to Micron's growing list of long-term supply contracts, many of which have minimum purchase commitments and price floors. Those contracts provide greater visibility into future revenue and may reduce the severity of future downturns.

Micron CEO Sanjay Mehrotra believes those contracts will make the company's financial performance more resilient. "These multi-year take-or-pay agreements sharpen our long-term supply planning and enhance the durability and predictability of our strong financial performance," he told analysts on the latest earnings call.

Wall Street estimates Micron's earnings will increase at 65% annually through fiscal 2028, according to LSEG. That makes the current valuation of 10 times adjusted earnings look cheap. But there is a major caveat: The last memory chip cycle peaked in late 2021, and Micron traded around 6 times adjusted earnings by late 2022.

In that context, the current valuation of 10 times adjusted earnings could drop sharply once the current memory cycle peaks. However, I think that possibility has already been priced into the stock. Micron trades around 4.4 times projected earnings for fiscal 2027.

So what? If Wall Street's earnings estimate is correct and Micron trades at 6 times adjusted earnings after reporting fourth-quarter financial results for fiscal 2027 (which usually happens in late September), the stock will trade at $1,045 per share in late 2027. That implies 37% upside from its current share price of $760.

How to find your IP address

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It's quick and easy, if you know where to look.

If you're looking for a quick answer to "What is my IP address?", a simple solution is to go to WhatIsMyIP.com and get it there. But the real answer is a little more complicated, because your devices don't have just one IP address. Every device on your network gets a private one from your router, and your whole home shares a single public one from your internet provider. The public address is the one websites see, while the private one only matters inside your own network.

Your router does the translating between the two, which is how a phone, a laptop and a TV can all browse at once while looking like a single address to the outside world. The router also hands out the private ones through a system called DHCP that usually lives inside the router itself. So the real answer depends on whether you're looking for your public address or one of your private ones.

Open a browser and go to DuckDuckGo. Type "what is my IP" into the search bar, and there it is. The answer shows up at the top of the results. Sites like WhatIsMyIP do the same thing and add your approximate location and your provider's name.

If you'd rather stay out of the browser, there's a faster way on both Mac and Windows. Open Terminal on a Mac, or Command Prompt on Windows, type the following and press Enter:

It produces your public IP and nothing else. If you're after your private IP instead, here's how.

On Windows 11, right-click the network icon in the taskbar and choose Network and Internet settings. If you are on Wi-Fi, click Wi-Fi, then click the network you're connected to. If you are connected through Ethernet, click Ethernet instead. Scroll down past the toggles until you reach a long list of technical details. Most of them have IPv6 in the name, and you can ignore those. Look for the line called IPv4 address. The numbers next to it, four groups separated by dots, are your private IP. Windows 10 is nearly the same, though on Wi-Fi you may need to click Hardware properties first.

You can do the exact same thing by opening Command Prompt and typing ipconfig. It works on every version of Windows, and the address is on the IPv4 Address line under your active connection.

On a Mac, the quickest route is to hold down Option and click the Wi-Fi icon in the menu bar. Your IP address is listed there, along with your router's. The longer way works for wired connections too: Apple menu > System Settings > Network. Then pick your connection, click Details, and look under the TCP/IP tab.

On an iPhone, go to Settings, tap Wi-Fi, then tap the i next to your network and scroll down to the IP address. Android works the same way on many phones: open Settings, tap Wi-Fi, then tap the i or the gear icon next to your network and look for IPv4. On a Pixel, the path is Settings > Network & internet > Internet > gear icon > Network details. Manufacturers move things around, so your phone may differ a little.

The router has its own address, and it's the one you type into a browser to reach its settings page. Your device lists it as the Default Gateway (or just Router), and it's often 192.168.1.1, 192.168.0.1 or 10.0.0.1. On Windows, it's right there in the ipconfig output.

Mostly for troubleshooting. If your connection is laggy or dead and a restart didn't help, your provider may ask for your device's IP to work out whether the problem is on your end or theirs. It also comes up when you're setting up a printer, a game server or remote access to a machine at home.

Your public IP probably won't stay put. Most home connections use dynamic addresses, which can change when you restart your modem or router. Static ones exist, but they're mostly for servers, such as the ones hosting big websites or running email.

A common question is whether you can look up someone else's IP. The answer is no, not from your end. Random people on the internet can't see yours, and a public IP usually points to a whole network, not one person or device. Websites and advertisers can see it, though, and sometimes use it to build a picture of what you like. A VPN hides it from them, and a proxy does too, some proxies do not encrypt your traffic, and a VPN always does. If you want to try one, you can check out this guide on how to use a VPN.

Catanzaro-Palermo, lavori in ritardo al Ceravolo: tribune ancora chiuse

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Non arrivano buone notizie sul fronte dei lavori al 'Ceravolo'. Il conto alla rovescia verso Catanzaro-Palermo, in programma sabato prossimo, è infatti accompagnato da nuove difficoltà legate al cantiere per il restyling dell'impianto calabrese. Le tempistiche inizialmente prospettate dall'amministrazione comunale non saranno rispettate e la riapertura delle tribune, auspicata per la sfida contro i rosanero, rischia di saltare.

Come riportato dalla Gazzetta del Sud, il rallentamento degli interventi sulla Tribuna Ovest renderà impossibile rispettare il programma annunciato dal sindaco Nicola Fiorita durante gli ultimi aggiornamenti sullo stato dei lavori. L'obiettivo era quello di restituire ai tifosi una parte più ampia dell'impianto proprio in occasione del ritorno casalingo del Catanzaro dopo la sosta per le nazionali. Gli interventi, però, procedono più lentamente del previsto, rendendo necessario individuare una nuova soluzione per organizzare l'accesso del pubblico.

Sono diverse le possibilità attualmente al vaglio per garantire lo svolgimento della partita in condizioni di sicurezza. Una delle soluzioni più concrete prevede di riservare ai sostenitori del Palermo una porzione della Tribuna Centrale, con una disponibilità massima stimata intorno ai 250 posti. Per quanto riguarda il pubblico di casa, si valuta invece la possibilità di delimitare la Tribuna Est e destinarla ai tifosi giallorossi, insieme alla Curva Est. Non è escluso, tuttavia, un secondo scenario che comporterebbe una riduzione complessiva della capienza di circa 2.000 posti rispetto alla gara interna contro il Padova. In questo caso i sostenitori del Catanzaro verrebbero concentrati principalmente nella Curva Mammì e nel settore Distinti, mentre ai tifosi ospiti sarebbe comunque riservata una porzione della Tribuna Centrale.

La distribuzione definitiva dei settori dovrà tenere conto anche delle prescrizioni delle autorità competenti e delle necessarie misure di sicurezza.

La giornata di domani sarà importante per definire il quadro organizzativo della sfida. Sono infatti attesi gli aggiornamenti degli organismi preposti alla sicurezza, con il CASMS chiamato a pronunciarsi anche sulle modalità di accesso dei sostenitori ospiti. Soltanto successivamente sarà possibile avere un quadro definitivo sulla disponibilità dei posti e sulla distribuzione del pubblico all'interno dell'impianto.

Il problema, dunque, non riguarda soltanto la partita contro il Palermo. Il ritardo degli interventi continua a condizionare la quotidianità del Catanzaro e rischia di riproporsi anche per i successivi impegni casalinghi, a partire dalla gara contro l'Avellino del 30 novembre. Una situazione che la società e l'amministrazione comunale dovranno continuare a monitorare, nella speranza di accelerare un progetto fondamentale per il futuro dello stadio e per tutta la tifoseria giallorossa.

Jensen Huang is discussing a return to Nvidia, FT reports

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Two big Nvidia investors want the board to keep the door open. The Treasury Secretary wants an outsider instead.

Jensen Huang at Nvidia’s GTC Berlin keynote, 21 October 2026.

Jensen Huang has been discussing a possible comeback as Nvidia’s chief executive, days after the board pushed him out, the Financial Times reported. Bloomberg relayed the report on Sunday.

Huang resigned on Thursday. He had admitted an affair with Alice Huang, whom US officials suspect of spying for China, and was reported to have had another with an Nvidia vice president. The two Huangs are not related, and Beijing denies the spying claims.

Several Silicon Valley allies have begun lobbying for his return, the FT said. Two of them are Nvidia investors. In a letter to the board posted on X, Altimeter’s Brad Gerstner and Atreides’ Gavin Baker urged a slow and deliberate approach to choosing a successor.

That approach should include “the potential return of Jensen Huang,” they wrote.

Washington is pulling the other way. On Sunday, Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent told Fox News that Nvidia needs a dramatic shakeup in its leadership. He said it should look outside the company for its next chief.

Bessent has made the scandal about chips, not private life. On Friday he said anyone at Nvidia should be held accountable if its chips leaked to China.

A return would also have to survive the board’s own inquiry, which is run by Wachtell Lipton. Huang is expected to testify before Congress, and Nvidia reports results on Tuesday under interim chief executive Colette Kress.

SNL gathers all the tech tycoons for a lesson in dating and spycraft

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

While awkward, creepy tech executives have become a recurring theme on Saturday Night Live, this week’s episode gave the cast a chance to show off all their impressions at once.

In a single sketch, we got James Austin Johnson as Peter Thiel, Ben Marshall as Sam Altman, Jane Wickline as Dario Amodei, Jeremy Culhane as Elon Musk, Andrew Dismukes as Mark Zuckerberg — and eventually, the recently departed Bowen Yang joined the fun as Jensen Huang.

Huang’s ouster from Nvidia (after admitting to an affair with a suspected Chinese spy) could have serious implications for the tech industry; it also provided a hook for some easy jokes about rich nerds being easy marks for women (or men) who are out of their league.

In the sketch, a CIA agent played by host John Mulaney (hilarious as ever, if not entirely confident about how to pronounce “Palantir” or “Nvidia”) attempts to painstakingly explain why these execs should be suspicious when someone attractive suddenly seems interested in them romantically. For Altman and Thiel’s sake, he added, “I hope this is obvious: Even though you’re gay, you can still be tricked by a spy.”

Johnson’s version of Thiel pushed back, insisting that that tech tycoons deserve more respect, given all they’ve done for the country. “Name one time technology has let us down,” he said, giving Mulaney a chance to remind viewers about flights being halted all across the United States on Friday night.

“Okay, name one more time,” the fictional Thiel said, leading to an obligatory mention of Microsoft Outlook going down on Thursday. When asked for a third example, Mulaney said, “No, I’m done naming times.”

“Look, I get it,” he said. “I can’t imagine how disorienting this has been for you, to go from being bug-eyed weirdos to being gods. I mean, never in the history of our species have people as pale and as strange as you a) survived, and b) been given all the world’s wealth and power.”

Amazon is reportedly working on a database for 'virtually every item for sale on Earth'

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This would include products that the online commerce giant doesn’t even sell.

Amazon is reportedly working on a way to compile all the product data in the world for online shopping in a project called the World Product Graph, according to Reuters. As first reported by Reuters, the online commerce giant has an internal document that details the World Product Graph, which was corroborated by "people familiar with the matter." The initiative will reportedly collect data on every item that's sold online, even if Amazon doesn't offer it.

Even for products not sold through Amazon, the World Product Graph would result in online shoppers still interacting with Amazon since its database would provide useful information many customers seek out before purchasing, including customer reviews. If released, the idea is that Amazon's World Product Graph would remove the need for online shoppers to visit Reddit or Google for this type of data, meaning more time spent on the Amazon ecosystem.

As a result of creating such a comprehensive database of online product info, Amazon could also increase the utility of its AI shopping agent, Alexa for Shopping, which replaced its predecessor Rufus. At the same time, creating the World Product Graph could be Amazon planting a flag in this data-heavy space, which would eventually see rivaling AI shopping agents relying on the tech giant's product info repository to provide answers to user prompts. According to an internal document that Reuters reviewed, Amazon already enlisted the help of Coca-Cola, Bayer and other manufacturers to provide product data.

Reuters said that it's unsure if and when the World Product Graph would be implemented, but reported that the document included an "aspirational future release date of October 1, 2027." An Amazon spokesperson told Reuters that "draft, outdated documents often paint an incomplete picture and the existence of a brainstorming document ​such as this one doesn't necessarily reflect our current plans."

How you can give your old Apple TV new purpose

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There are a few other tasks your Apple TV can handle.

If you've upgraded your Apple TV 4K to the latest generation, it's really best to repurpose the old device if you can. As streaming add-ons go, the product has always been overbuilt, no matter that you're now paying $300 for the privilege. Tossing out an old Apple TV because you've got a replacement is a bit like tossing a Swiss Army knife because the new one has a few extra tools.

The catch is that an Apple TV isn't a generic Android box. It's running tvOS on custom Apple silicon, so you can't just flash it and run whatever software you like. If you're going to reuse it, you have to get creative, working within Apple's walled garden.

This is where the device's excessive power comes in handy. There are a few things you can do with an older model, ranging from the obvious — moving it to a secondary TV — to using it to anchor your other smart home accessories.

If you've got any secondary TVs in your home, even a 2017 Apple TV might be an upgrade. I find tvOS to be a lot smoother to use than its rivals, partly because it has far fewer ads or forced recommendations. It's probably a great upgrade for a child's TV, or that small set you just mounted in your garage gym — where the main concern is going to be loading Rocky IV as quickly as possible, not whether you can watch with HDR10+ and Dolby Atmos.

It's also worth remembering that unlike other streaming devices, an Apple TV 4K can run games you'd actually want to play, including console-style titles like Dead Cells, Inside or Wreckfest. You might intentionally buy a small TV or monitor for it. It's wise to pair an Xbox or PlayStation controller, though, since few games work well with just the Siri Remote. The big exception to this is the many Jackbox party games available on the platform, which players can join simply by visiting a website on their phone. If you haven't tried one, I highly recommend it.

Another secret weapon is AirPlay. If you've got an iPhone, iPad or Mac, you can use AirPlay to mirror screen content to a TV in order to get work (or fun) done on a much larger canvas. There are a few important notes to make, however. First, an Apple TV may be redundant in this regard, since a lot of TVs now support AirPlay out of the box. Second, there's inherent lag in wireless casting, so AirPlay isn't going to be a viable option for action games like Resident Evil 4. Lastly, AirPlay mirroring doesn't preserve features like Dolby Vision, so you should always stream movies and shows locally on an Apple TV if you can.

One particularly good reason to leave an Apple TV connected, even if you never switch inputs, is to have another Home Hub for HomeKit, Apple's smart home platform. HomeKit is technically usable without a Hub — but having one might as well be mandatory, since it's what enables remote access and allows automations to trigger 24/7.

While you can also use HomePods and HomeViews for this purpose, the more Hubs you have, the better. That's because one can take over if another disconnects or needs to be updated. Although these problems may not be frequent, that's cold comfort if you're counting on HomeKit to adjust the temperature or trigger room lighting.

Apple TVs also serve as Matter controllers, allowing them to pair Matter-ready accessories with HomeKit. Second-, fourth- and some third-generation models can act additionally act as Thread border routers, bridging compatible accessories with each other and the internet. You most definitely want as many Thread devices in your home as possible, because the more you have, the more resilient and responsive the Thread mesh network becomes. That could count for a lot if Apple doesn't lose its reborn interest in smart home tech.

Microsoft Copilot AI Predicts a Seismic Hyperliquid Rally by 2027

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Hyperliquid is trading near $65–$68 in mid-November 2026 after pulling back with the broader market. In an over-the-top bullish scenario, Microsoft Copilot AI predicts that HYPE could still deliver one of the more explosive moves among major DeFi assets by January 1, 2027.

The landmark catalyst driving this extreme upside is a highly unlikely but not impossible regulatory and institutional breakthrough.

In this hypothetical, Hyperliquid successfully obtains a major U.S. regulatory designation that allows its perpetual futures platform (or a closely affiliated entity) to operate as a fully compliant, onshore derivatives venue accessible to a wide range of traditional finance participants.

Combined with a surge in institutional volume migrating onto the chain and aggressive token utility expansions (such as enhanced staking, revenue sharing, or treasury mechanisms), HYPE experiences a structural demand shock that re-rates the entire project far beyond current valuations.

HYPE has corrected sharply from its late-October highs near $95–$96 and is now consolidating in the mid-$60s after testing support around $62–$64. The recent decline has formed a series of lower highs, with immediate resistance clustered between $72 and $78 and a more significant supply zone near the prior peak.

On the downside, the $60–$62 area represents the first major demand zone; a sustained break below it would open the path toward the $50 region. Momentum indicators have cooled considerably after the sell-off, leaving the asset oversold on intermediate timeframes and creating room for a sharp reversal if buying pressure returns.

In a standard recovery, a weekly close back above $75–$80 would signal that the corrective phase is ending. Under the extreme regulatory and institutional catalyst outlined above, those intermediate levels would be cleared rapidly, allowing measured-move extensions and multi-cycle projections from the current base to support a far more aggressive upside trajectory.

While present price action reflects broader risk-off conditions and recent liquidations, Hyperliquid’s position as a high-volume perpetual exchange continues to give it a distinct profile among large-cap tokens.

The combination of its existing product-market fit and the theoretical potential for a transformative regulatory green light creates a setup from which a parabolic move remains possible.

Microsoft Copilot AI predicts that if everything aligns, HYPE could reach somewhere between $280 and $380 by January 1, 2027, with a stretch target above $450 in the most aggressive outcome.

This remains pure speculation and entertainment, not a realistic base-case outlook. The catalyst would require extraordinary regulatory progress and execution for Hyperliquid at a scale that is currently highly unlikely.
